A.10
) by the maximum principle, and (
A.8
) follows.
∎

We now present the

proof of Proposition
6
.

Existence and uniqueness of a weak solution follows by applying Lemma
21
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{(y_{0},y_{\bar{a}})\}

for a.e.
(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T)
.

Next, we fix
y_{0}\in(0,T)
, apply (
A.7
) on a.e.
y_{\bar{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}
, square both sides, and integrate with respect to
y_{\bar{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}
. At this
y_{0}
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{a}\psi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{a}^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}\|\psi\|^{2}_{H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{y_{\bar{a}}\})}dy_{\bar{a}}

(A.12)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}\quad\mbox{ when \eqref{scalar.Rd} holds.}

Now let
r
be a multiindex on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that
|r|=l\leq k
.
We first assume that
\eta
and
q
are smooth, and hence
\psi
is smooth.
We can then differentiate (
A.6
) to find that

\displaystyle(-\Delta_{a}+|\varphi(q)|^{2})\partial^{r}\psi

\displaystyle=\partial^{r}\eta+|\varphi(q)|^{2}\partial^{r}\psi-\partial^{r}(|\varphi(q)|^{2}\psi)

(A.13)

We now estimate the
L^{2}
norm of the right-hand side. We use Lemma
24
below
to estimate

\displaystyle\||\varphi(q)|^{2}\partial^{r}\psi-\partial^{r}(|\varphi(q)|^{2}\psi)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{r_{1}+r_{2}=r,|r_{1}|\geq 1}\|\partial^{r_{1}}\varphi(q)\partial^{r_{2}}\psi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{f(q)psi}}{\leq}C(q)\|\psi\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

This combined
15
15

15

We are not using the full strength of (
A.12
) because we will not need it, and to simplify notation in what follows.
with (
A.12
) and (
A.13
) implies that

\|\partial^{r}\psi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\|\partial^{r}\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\psi\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

Since this holds for all
r
with
|r|=l
, it follows that

\|\psi\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\psi\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

for
l\leq k
. Then an easy induction argument implies that

\|\psi\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

Since this holds for all
l\leq k
and for all
t\in(0,T)
,

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.14)

We next claim that if
\eta\in L^{\infty}_{T}W^{k-j,\infty}_{\gamma}

then
\psi
belongs to the same space and

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{k-j,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{k-j,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.15)

The proof is very similar to that of (
A.14
):
we apply the 2d estimate (
A.8
) to (
A.13
) on

{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{(y_{a},y_{0})\}
for
a.e.

(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})
, using an induction argument and the exponential decay of derivatives of
\eta
,
i.e.
the finiteness of the right-hand side of (
A.15
), to verify that the right-hand side satisfies (
A.9
).

Combining (
A.14
) and (
A.15
), we obtain

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.16)

Finally, note that
\partial_{0}\psi
satisfies

(-\Delta_{a}+|\varphi(q)|^{2})\partial_{0}\psi=\partial_{0}\eta-\partial_{0}|\varphi(q)|^{2}\psi\in L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k-1,j}_{\gamma}\quad\mbox{ if }\kappa_{0}>0.

Applying (
A.16
) to this equation, we obtain the conclusion (
A.5
) of the Proposition.

If
\eta
and
q
have only the regularity assumed in the theorem, equation (
A.13
) can be justified by an approximation argument or by difference quotients and induction, and the rest of the proof is unchanged.
∎

A.2.
An elliptic system

In this subsection we consider the system

L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]\tilde{u}=\eta\qquad\mbox{ on }{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times(0,T)

(A.17)

for
\tilde{u},\eta:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times(0,T)\to{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, where
q:{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T)\to M_{N}
is a map satisfying (
A.2
) and (
A.3
).
One may understand (
A.17
) as a
d-1
-parameter family of equations on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
:

L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]\tilde{u}=\eta\qquad\qquad\mbox{on ${\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})}:={\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})\}$}

for every
(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T)
.
The natural solvability condition for the equation is

\eta\perp\ker(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]).

(A.18)

In view of Stuart’s Theorem
6
, this can be written more explicitly as

(\eta,n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})})}=0\quad\mbox{ for all }\mu=1,\ldots,2N\mbox{ and }(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T).

(A.19)

Below is our main result about this system.

Proposition 7
.

Fix
j,k
such that
\frac{d}{2}<j+1\leq k\leq L
, and assume that
\eta:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times(0,T)\to{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

satisfies (
A.18
) and (
A.4
) for some
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
.

Then (
A.17
) has a unique solution
\tilde{u}=\binom{\tilde{\Phi}}{\tilde{A}}
with the property that

\tilde{u}\perp\ker(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)])

(A.20)

and there exists a constant
C
, depending on
K,\Theta_{q}
and
\gamma
, such that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\kappa_{0}\|\partial_{0}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k-1,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\Theta_{\eta}.

(A.21)

Moreover, if
\eta(\cdot,y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})
satisfies the gauge-orthogonality condition at
\mathfrak{u}(q(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0}))
for every
(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})
:

\partial_{a}\eta_{a}(y)-(i\varphi(y_{a};q(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})),\eta_{\phi}(y)=0

then so does
\tilde{u}
:

\partial_{a}\tilde{A}_{a}(y)-(i\varphi(y_{a};q(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})),\tilde{\Phi}(y))=0.

(A.22)

We start with a 2d lemma.

Lemma 22
.

Let
K
be a compact subset of
M_{N}
. Assume that
q\in K
, and that
\eta\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2};{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
satisfies

(\eta,n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}=0\qquad\mbox{ for }\mu=1,\ldots,2N

Then there exists a unique
\tilde{u}=\binom{\tilde{\Phi}}{\tilde{A}_{a}}\in H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2};{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
satisfying

L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u})=\eta,\qquad\qquad(\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}=0\ \ \mbox{ for }\mu=1,\ldots,2N.

(A.23)

Moreover,
\tilde{u}
satisfies

\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}

(A.24)

and if
\eta
satisfies the gauge-orthogonality condition at
\mathfrak{u}(q)

\partial_{a}\eta_{a}-(i\varphi(q),\eta_{\phi})=0

then so does
\tilde{u}
:

\partial_{a}\tilde{A}_{a}-(i\varphi(q),\tilde{\Phi})=0.

(A.25)

Finally, if

\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}:=\operatorname{ess\,sup_{y_{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}}(|\eta(y_{a})|e^{\gamma|y_{a}|})<\infty

(A.26)

for some
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
, then

\tilde{u}\in L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, and

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}},\qquad C=C(K,\gamma).

(A.27)

Proof.

Existence of a unique weak solution
\tilde{u}
satisfying the orthogonality condition
(\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}=0

for
\mu=1,\ldots,2N
follows directly from Theorem
6

via standard arguments using the Riesz representation theorem in the Hilbert space

\{\tilde{u}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2};{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})\ :\ \ (\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}=0\mbox{ for all }\mu=1,\ldots,2N\}

equipped with the
H^{1}
norm. This solution satisfies
\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}
. Basic elliptic regularity implies that
\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})})
, and then (
A.24
) follows.

Next, assume that
\eta
satisfies the gauge-orthogonality condition, which
implies that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\eta\cdot\binom{i\varphi(q)\xi}{\partial_{a}\xi}\ =0\qquad\mbox{ for all }\xi\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}).

It follows from (
A.23
) that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u})\cdot\binom{i\varphi(q)\xi}{\partial_{a}\xi}\ =0\qquad\mbox{ for all }\xi\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}).

Then (
2.27
) implies that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}{\mathcal{L}}[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u})\cdot\binom{i\varphi(q)\xi}{\partial_{a}\xi}\ =-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\binom{i\varphi(q)(\nabla\cdot\tilde{A}-(i\varphi(q),\tilde{\Phi})}{\partial_{a}(\nabla\cdot\tilde{A}-(i\varphi(q),\tilde{\Phi})}\cdot\binom{i\varphi(q)\xi}{\partial_{a}\xi}.

for all
\xi\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. But the left-hand side vanishes, by (
2.26
) and the fact that
{\mathcal{L}}[\mathfrak{u}(q)]
is self-adjoint, which follows from the fact that
{\mathcal{L}}
is the second variation of an energy functional.
So

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\binom{i\varphi(q)(\nabla\cdot\tilde{A}-(i\varphi(q),\tilde{\Phi})}{\partial_{a}(\nabla\cdot\tilde{A}-(i\varphi(q),\tilde{\Phi})}\cdot\binom{i\varphi(q)\xi}{\partial_{a}\xi}=0\ .

Taking
\xi=\partial_{a}\tilde{A}_{a}-(i\varphi(q),\tilde{\Phi})
, we conclude that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}|\nabla\xi|^{2}+|\varphi(q)|^{2}\xi^{2}=0

and hence (by Lemma
18
) that
\xi=0
, which says exactly that
\tilde{u}
is gauge-orthogonal.

Finally, assume that
\eta
satisfies (
A.26
). The embedding
H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\hookrightarrow C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
implies that
\tilde{u}(y_{a})\to 0
as
|y_{a}|\to\infty
, with

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}

(A.28)

for
C=C(K,\gamma)
.
Recalling that
\partial_{a}\mathcal{A}_{a}(q)=0
, we can rewrite the system
L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u})

in the form

\displaystyle-\Delta\tilde{\Phi}+2i\mathcal{A}_{a}(q)\partial_{a}\tilde{\Phi}+\left(\frac{1}{2}(3|\varphi(q)|^{2}-1)+|\mathcal{A}(q)|^{2}\right)\tilde{\Phi}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\phi}-2i(D_{\mathcal{A}_{a}}\varphi)(q)\tilde{A}_{a}

(A.29)

\displaystyle-\Delta\tilde{A}_{a}+|\varphi(q)|^{2}\tilde{A}_{a}

\displaystyle=\eta_{a}+2(i\tilde{\Phi},D_{\mathcal{A}_{a}}\varphi(q)).

It follows from (
2.8
) that
|D_{\mathcal{A}_{a}}\varphi(y_{a};q)|\leq Ce^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}
, so
the desired exponential decay of
\tilde{A}_{a}
for
a=1,2
is a consequences of Lemma (
21
), with the same decay rate as
\eta
.

Next, we derive a differential inequality satisfied by
|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}
.
To do this we note that

\Delta|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}=2(\Delta\tilde{\Phi},\tilde{\Phi})+2|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}

From (
A.29
), (
2.8
) and (
A.26
), we check that

(\Delta\tilde{\Phi},\tilde{\Phi})\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}(3|\varphi(q)|^{2}-1)+|\mathcal{A}(q)|^{2}\right)|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+(2i\mathcal{A}(q)_{a}\partial_{a}\tilde{\Phi},\tilde{\Phi})-Ce^{-\gamma|y_{a}}|\tilde{\Phi}|.

Noting that

|(2i\mathcal{A}(q)_{a}\partial_{a}\tilde{\Phi},\tilde{\Phi})|\leq|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{A}(q)|^{2}\,|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2},

we deduce that

\Delta|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\geq(3|\varphi(q)|^{2}-1)|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-Ce^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}|\tilde{\Phi}|.

Since
\gamma<1/2
and
|\varphi(y_{a};q)|^{2}\to 1
exponentially fast as
|y_{a}|\to\infty
, we can find
R=R(\gamma,K)
such that

3|\varphi(q)|^{2}-1>1+\gamma
whenever
|y_{a}|\geq R
. In this region,

\Delta|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\geq 2\gamma|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+(1-\gamma)|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-Ce^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}|\tilde{\Phi}|\geq 2\gamma|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-Ce^{-2\gamma|y_{a}|}.

We now define
v=|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-Ke^{-2\gamma|y_{a}|}
for
K
to be chosen below. We use the above inequality to compute

\Delta v\geq 2\gamma|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-\left(C+4\gamma^{2}K\right)e^{-2\gamma|y_{a}|}=2\gamma v+(2\gamma K-C-4\gamma^{2}K)e^{-2\gamma|y_{a}|}.

Since
\gamma<1/2
, and recalling (
A.28
), we can choose
K
large enough that

\Delta v\geq 2\gamma v,\mbox{ when }|y_{a}|>R,\qquad\mbox{ and }v(y_{a})<0\mbox{ when }|y_{a}|=R.

Then recalling that
\tilde{u}\to 0
as
|y_{a}|\to\infty
, a maximum principle argument shows that
v\leq 0
, and hence that (
A.27
) holds, for
|y_{a}|\geq R
. The same inequality for
|y_{a}|\leq R
follows from (
A.28
), after adjusting constants if necessary. This proves (
A.27
).
∎

We now present the

proof of Proposition
7
.

We follow the proof of Proposition
6
, with adjustments to account for complications such as the fact that
L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]
has a nontrivial kernel.

Existence and uniqueness of a weak solution follows by applying Lemma
22
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})}

for a.e.
(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T)
. The assertion about gauge-orthogonality follows from applying the corresponding claim from Lemma
22
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})}
for every
y_{\bar{a}}
.

Next we fix
y_{0}\in(0,T)
, and having done so we write for example
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{y_{a}}
in place of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{(y_{\bar{a}},y_{0})}
, and
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
instead of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{y_{0}\}
. This
y_{0}
will remain fixed
until we consider
\partial_{0}\tilde{u}
, near the end of the proof.

We apply (
A.24
) on a.e.
y_{\bar{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}
, square both sides, and integrate with respect to
y_{\bar{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}
.

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}\|\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{y_{\bar{a}}\})}dy_{\bar{a}}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}\quad\mbox{ when \eqref{system.Rd} holds.}

(A.30)

Now let
r
be a multiindex on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that
|r|=l\leq k
.
We first assume that
\eta
and
q
are smooth, and hence
\tilde{u}
is smooth.
We can then differentiate (
A.17
) to find that

\displaystyle L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\partial^{r}\tilde{u})

\displaystyle=\partial^{r}\eta+L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\partial^{r}\tilde{u})-\partial^{r}(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u}))

(A.31)

We write

\partial^{r}\tilde{u}=w^{\perp}_{r}+w^{\parallel}_{r},\qquad\mbox{ where }w^{\perp}_{r}\perp\ker(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]).

(A.32)

It is easy to see that

w^{\parallel}_{r}(y)=(\partial^{r}\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{y_{\bar{a}}})}n_{\mu}(q)

(A.33)

where
\{n_{\mu}(\cdot;q)\}
is the orthonormal basis fixed in Definition
2
, and we sum implicitly
\mu=1,\ldots,2N
.
By differentiating the orthogonality condition (
A.19
), we find that

(\partial^{r}\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}(y_{\bar{a}}))}=\sum_{r_{1}+r_{2}=r,|r_{2}|\geq 1}\frac{r!}{r_{1}!r_{2}!}(\partial^{r_{1}}\tilde{u},\partial^{r_{2}}n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}(y_{\bar{a}}))}

(A.34)

We use Lemma
24
below to estimate

\displaystyle\|w^{\parallel}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}\left((\partial^{r}\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}(y_{\bar{a}}))}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}|n_{\mu}(y_{a};q(y_{\bar{a}}))|^{2}dy_{a}\right)dy_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}(\partial^{r}\tilde{u},n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}(y_{\bar{a}}))}^{2}dy_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}\sum_{r_{1}+r_{2}=r,|r_{2}|\geq 1}(\partial^{r_{1}}\tilde{u},\partial^{r_{2}}n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}(y_{\bar{a}}))}^{2}dy_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{f(q)psi}}{\leq}C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}.

(A.35)

In fact, since
\|n_{\mu}(q)\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\leq C(k,K)
for
q\in K
, the same argument shows that

\|w^{\parallel}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{a}w^{\parallel}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{a}^{2}w^{\parallel}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}\leq C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2},

(A.36)

where
\nabla_{a}
denotes the gradient
(\partial_{y_{1}},\partial_{y_{2}})
with respect to the
y_{a}
variables.

To control
w^{\perp}_{r}
, note first that
L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](w^{\parallel}_{r})=0
by construction, so
(
A.31
) implies that

L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](w_{r}^{\perp})=\partial^{r}\eta+L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\partial^{r}\tilde{u})-\partial^{r}(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u})).

(A.37)

We now estimate the
L^{2}
norm of the commutator term on the the right-hand side. First note from the definition (
2.28
) that for every
q
,

L_{1}[\mathfrak{u}(q)]:=L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]-\Delta_{a}

is a first-order differential operator involving only
\partial_{y_{a}}
derivatives whose coefficients are smooth functions of
y_{a}
and
q(y_{\bar{a}})
. It is clear that

L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\partial^{r}\tilde{u})-\partial^{r}(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u}))=L_{1}[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\partial^{r}\tilde{u})-\partial^{r}(L_{1}[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u}))

(A.38)

The right-hand side is a sum of terms of the form

\partial^{r_{1}}f(q)\partial^{r_{2}}\tilde{u},\qquad\partial^{r_{1}}f(q)\partial^{r_{2}}\partial_{a}\tilde{u},

(A.39)

with
r_{1}+r_{2}=r
and
|r_{1}|\geq 1
, where
f(q)
is a function of the type considered in Lemma
24
.
Indeed, every
f(q)
is is a low-degree (at most 2) polynomial in components of
\mathfrak{u}(y_{a};q(y_{\bar{a}}))
or their first
\partial_{y_{a}}
derivatives,
and all components of
\mathfrak{u}(y_{a};q)
are smooth with respect to both

y_{a}
and
q
, as noted in Lemma
4
. Thus Lemma
24
implies that every coefficient function
f(q)
above
satisfies

\|\partial^{r_{1}}f(q)\partial^{r_{2}}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C(\Theta)\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

Thus, arguing as in the proof of Proposition
6
, we deduce that

\|L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\partial^{r}\tilde{u})-\partial^{r}(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)](\tilde{u}))\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C(q)\left(\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\|\nabla_{a}\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\right).

Recalling (
A.37
), the basic estimate (
A.30
) implies that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{a}^{2}w^{\perp}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\|\nabla_{a}w^{\perp}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\|w^{\perp}_{r}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial^{r}\eta\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\nabla_{a}\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

We combine this with (
A.36
). Since the resulting inequality
holds for all
r
with
|r|=l
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\|\nabla_{a}\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\|\nabla_{a}^{2}\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\nabla_{a}\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

for
l\leq k
. An easy induction argument, with the
l=0
case supplied by (
A.30
), shows that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{H^{l}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

Since this holds at arbitrary
y_{0}
and for all
l\leq k
, it follows that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.40)

We next claim that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{k-j,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{k-j,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.41)

We assume by induction that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l-1,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l-1,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

for
1\leq l\leq j-k
.
For a multiindex
r
such that
|r|=l
, we decompose
\partial^{r}\tilde{u}=w^{\perp}_{r}+w^{\parallel}_{r}
as in (
A.32
).
In view of (
A.33
), (
A.34
) and properties of
n_{\mu}
from Lemma
4
, to estimate

\|w^{\parallel}_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
it suffices to show that if
|r_{1}|\leq l-1
then

\mbox{ $\psi(y_{0},y_{\bar{a}}):=(\partial^{r_{1}}\tilde{u},\partial^{r_{2}}n_{\mu})_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{(y_{0},y_{\bar{a}})})}$ satisfies }\ \|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l-1,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

But this follows immediately from the induction hypothesis and properties of
n_{\mu}
.
We then conclude that

\|w^{\parallel}_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l-1,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\qquad\mbox{ if }|r|=l.

Next, we write
w^{\perp}_{r}=(\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{r},\tilde{A}^{\perp}_{r,a})
.
We write the equation satisfied by
w^{\perp}_{r}
as in (
A.37
), (
A.38
), (
A.39
). From the explicit form (
A.29
) of
L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]
, we see that the
a=1,2
components of this system can be written

(-\Delta+|\varphi(q)|^{2})\tilde{A}^{\perp}_{r,a}=\eta_{r,a}+\mbox{ linear combination of terms of the form }\partial^{r_{1}}f(r)\partial^{r_{2}}\tilde{u}

with
|r_{2}|\leq l-1
. Using the induction hypothesis, we check that

\|\mbox{right-hand side }\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\ \leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

and then Lemma
21
implies that

\|\tilde{A}_{r,a}^{\perp}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

The right-hand side is finite, since we have assumed that
l\leq k-j
.

By inspection of (
A.29
), we see that the
\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{r}
components solve an equation of the form

\displaystyle-\Delta\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{r}+2i\mathcal{A}(q)_{a}\partial_{a}\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{r}+\sum_{|s|=l}\Xi_{s}\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{s}+\left(\frac{1}{2}(3|\varphi(q)|^{2}-1)+|\mathcal{A}(q)|^{2}\right)\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{r}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=\eta_{r,\phi}-\sum_{|s|=l}\Xi_{s}\tilde{\Phi}^{\parallel}_{s}

(A.42)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+\mbox{ linear combination of terms of the form }\partial^{r_{1}}f(r)\partial^{r_{2}}\tilde{u}

where
\sum_{s|=l}\Xi_{s}\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{s}
and
-\sum_{s|=l}\Xi_{s}\tilde{\Phi}^{\parallel}_{s}
collect all terms with
l-1
derivatives of
\partial_{a}\tilde{\Phi}
and one derivative of
A_{a}
that arise when expanding

\partial^{r}(2i\mathcal{A}_{a}(q)\partial_{a}\tilde{\Phi})
. Thus, each
\Xi_{s}
is either
0
or has the form
C2i\partial_{j}\mathcal{A}_{a}(q)
for some
C,a,j
and hence, by Lemma
4
, satisfies

|\Xi_{s}(y)|\leq C(1+|y_{a}|)^{-2}.

(A.43)

From the induction hypothesis and our earlier discussion of
w_{r}^{\parallel}
, the right-hand side of (
A.42
) is bounded by

\|\mbox{right-hand side}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

We define
\Psi=(\tilde{\Phi}^{\perp}_{r})_{r=l}
. Multiplying (
A.42
) by
\tilde{\Phi}_{r}
, summing over multiindices
r
such that
|r|=l
, using (
A.43
) and
arguing as in the proof of Lemma
22
, we find that

\Delta|\Psi|^{2}\geq(3|\varphi(q)|^{2}-1-C(1+|y_{a}|)^{-2})|\Psi|^{2}-Ce^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}|\Psi|.

One can then follow the proof of Lemma
22
to show that

|\Psi|^{2}\leq Ke^{-\gamma|y_{a}|},\qquad\mbox{ with }K\leq\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{l,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

This completes the induction argument and hence proves (
A.41
).

So far we have proved that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\ .

(A.44)

when
L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]\tilde{u}=\eta
. By applying (
A.44
) to the equation

L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]\partial_{0}\tilde{u}=\partial_{0}\eta+L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]\partial_{0}\tilde{u}-\partial_{0}(L[\mathfrak{u}(q)]\tilde{u})

and using (
A.44
) to estimate the right-hand side. we obtain

\|\partial_{0}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k-1,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\partial_{0}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k-1,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}X^{k,j}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\ .

This and (
A.44
) immediately imply the conclusion (
A.21
) of the Proposition,
∎

A.3.
A nonlinear evolution equation

In this section we recall standard facts about the system of equations
equation

(\kappa_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}+\kappa_{1}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}-\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}})f_{\nu}=rhs_{\nu},\qquad\nu=1,\ldots,2N

(A.45)

where
rhs_{\nu}
is a polynomial function of
f_{\mu},\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f_{\mu},\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}}f_{\mu}
, for
\mu=1,\ldots,2N
, with coefficients that are rather smooth functions, such that the estimate

\|rhs(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\left(1+\Theta_{1}(t)+\Theta_{1}(t)^{3}\right)

(A.46)

holds for all
t
when
f
is smooth enough, where

\Theta_{1}(t):=\|f(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{k+1}}+\kappa_{0}\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{k}}

(A.47)

The equation is posed on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T)
, and we assume that
k>\frac{d}{2}-1
.

The point of this discussion is to check that the regularity (
A.46
) the nonlinearity is sufficient to control the solution in the norms appearing in (
A.47
) above. This is presumably standard, but we do not know a handy reference.

Lemma 23
.

The system of equations (
A.45
) with initial data

(f,\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f)|_{t=0}=(0,0)
has a unique solution

f\in C(0,T_{0},H^{k+1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}))\quad\mbox{ with }\ \ \textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\in C(0,T_{0}),H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}))

(A.48)

for some
T_{0}\in(0,T)
that can be bounded below in terms of the constant in (
A.46
). This solution satisfies

\Theta_{1}(t)\leq\begin{cases}\sqrt{2Ct}&\mbox{ if }0\leq t\leq\frac{1}{2C}\\
(3-4Ct)^{-1/4}&\mbox{ if }\frac{1}{2C}\leq t<\min\{3t/4C,T\}\leq T_{0}.\end{cases}

(A.49)

Proof.

First we, we modify the system by adding
f_{\nu}
to both sides.
It becomes

(\kappa_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}+\kappa_{1}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}-\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}}+1)f_{\nu}=rhs_{\nu},\qquad\nu=1,\ldots,2N

(A.50)

for a new
rhs
that still satisfies (
A.46
), with the constant adjusted if necessary.

We next prove an
a priori
estimate for (
A.50
). Let
r
be a multiindex
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}
such that
|r|\leq k
. We apply
\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}
to (
A.45
), then multiply both sides of the equation by
\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}f_{\nu}
. After integration by parts and summing over
\nu
, this leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}\left(\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}\nabla_{\bar{a}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{0}\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\kappa_{1}\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}(rhs)|\ |\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}^{r}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f|

\displaystyle\leq\|rhs\|_{H^{k}}\,\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{H^{k}}.

Summing over all multiindices
r
such that
0\leq|r|\leq k
and appealing to (
A.46
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}(\Theta_{1}(t)^{2})+\kappa_{1}\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{H^{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\Theta_{1}(t)+\Theta_{1}(t)^{3})\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{H^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta}(1+\Theta_{1}^{6})+\delta\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{H^{k}}^{2}\quad\mbox{ for any }\delta>0.

Note also that
\Theta_{1}(0)=0
.

If
\kappa_{0}>0
then we take
\delta=1
and estimate
\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}
by
C(\kappa_{0})\Theta_{1}(t)^{2}
. If
\kappa_{0}=0
then
\kappa_{1}>0
and we can take
\delta=\kappa_{1}
. Either way we obtain

\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}(\Theta_{1}^{2})\leq C(1+\Theta_{1}^{6}),\qquad\Theta_{1}^{2}(0)=0.

The right-hand side is bounded by
2C\max\{1,2C\Theta_{1}^{6}\}
, from which it is easy to check that (
A.49
) holds.

Existence of solutions can be proved by the applying the contraction mapping principle in the space

Y:=\{f\in C(0,T_{1};H^{k+1}):\kappa_{0}f_{t}\in C(0,T_{1};H^{k})\}

for
T_{1}
to be chosen, with the natural norm

\|f\|_{Y}=\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}H^{k+1}}+\kappa_{0}\|\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}H^{k}}.

The mapping in question is given by

f\in Y\mapsto g\mbox{ solving }(\kappa_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}+\kappa_{1}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{0}-\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}}\textsf{\reflectbox{6}}_{\bar{a}})g=rhs(f)

with initial data specified.
The key point is that for
f_{1},f_{2}\in Y
, because
rhs
is at most cubic in
f
and its first derivatives,

\|rhs(f_{1})-rhs(f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}H^{k}}\leq C(\|f_{1}\|_{Y}^{2}+\|f_{2}\|_{Y}^{2})\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{Y}^{2}.

This combined with estimates like those above show that if
T_{1}
is small enough, the mapping is a contraction in a suitable ball in
Y
. This argument can be iterated to extend the interval of existence to at least
T_{0}
.
∎

A.4.
further lemmas

Lemma 24
.

Assume that
q:{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\to M_{N}
satisfies

\mbox{ $\exists$ compact }K\subset M_{N}\mbox{ such that }q(y_{\bar{a}})\in K\mbox{ for all }(y_{\bar{a}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}.

(A.51)

Assume that

\partial_{\bar{a}}q\in H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})
for some
L\geq d
, and set

\Theta:=\|\partial_{\bar{a}}q\|_{H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}.

Assume that
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times M_{N}\to{\mathbb{R}}
or
{\mathbb{C}}
is a function such that

|\partial^{r}f(y_{a},q)|\leq C(1+|y_{a}|)^{-|r|}\qquad\mbox{ whenever }q\in K

(A.52)

for any multi-index
r
denoting differentiation with respect to the
y_{a}
and
q
variables, where
C=C(r,K)
.
Let
f(q):{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
denote the function

f(q)(y):=f(y_{a};q(y_{\bar{a}})),

(A.53)

Then
f(q)\in W^{L+1-\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, and

\|f(q)\|_{W^{L+1-\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C.

(A.54)

Moreover if
k\leq L
then

\|f(q)\,\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

(A.55)

and for multiindices
r,s
such that
|r|+|s|\leq k
and
|r|\geq 1
,

\|\partial^{r}f(q)\,\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.56)

Finally, if

|\partial^{r}f(y_{a},q)|\leq C(1+|y_{a}|)^{-|r|-1}\qquad\mbox{ whenever }q\in K,

then

\|\partial_{\bar{a}}q\,f(q)\,\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\qquad\mbox{ for }k\leq L.

(A.57)

Proof.

To prove (
A.54
), we set
j=\lfloor\frac{d}{2}\rfloor>\frac{d}{2}-1
, and we fix a multiindex
|r|\leq L+1-j
. We write
r=r_{a}+r_{\bar{a}}
, where
\partial^{r_{a}}
contains all derivatives with respect to the
y_{a}
variables, and
\partial^{r_{\bar{a}}}
contains all derivatives with respect to
y_{\bar{a}}
. If
r=r_{a}
, then (
A.52
)
implies that

|\partial^{r}f(q)|(y)\leq C=C(k).

If not, it follows from the chain rule and (
A.52
) that

|\partial^{r}f(q)|(y)\leq C\sum_{l=1}^{|r_{\bar{a}}|}\sum_{r_{1}+\cdots+r_{l}=r_{\bar{a}}}\left((1+|y_{a}|)^{-l}\prod_{m=1}^{l}|\partial^{r_{m}}q|(y_{\bar{a}})\right).

(A.58)

Since
|r_{m}|\leq|r_{\bar{a}}|\leq L+1-j
and
j>\frac{d}{2}-1
,

\|\partial^{r_{m}}q\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\|\partial^{r_{m}}q\|_{H^{j}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\|\nabla q\|_{H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\Theta.

We obtain (
A.54
) directly from the previous two inequalities.

We next prove (
A.56
). Fix
r,s
such that
|r|+|s|\leq k\leq L
.
If
k\leq d/2
then
|r|\leq d/2\leq L+1-\lfloor d/2\rfloor
, since
L\geq d
, so
(
A.54
) implies that
\partial^{r}f(q)\in L^{\infty}
, and (
A.56
) is an
easy consequence.

We now assume that
k>\frac{d}{2}
,
and we write
r=r_{a}+r_{\bar{a}}
as above.
If
r=r_{a}
then

\|\partial^{r}f(q)\,\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\|\partial^{r_{a}}f(q)\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\overset{\eqref{fdecay}}{\leq}C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

So we may assume that
|r_{\bar{a}}|\geq 1
, in which case (
A.58
) holds.
It suffices to estimate the product of
\partial^{s}\tilde{u}
with a single term in the sum on the right-hand side, say

\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\prod_{m=1}^{l}Q_{r_{m}},\qquad\mbox{ for }Q_{r_{m}}(y):=|\partial^{r_{m}}q|(y_{\bar{a}})(1+|y_{a}|)^{-1},

where
|r_{1}|+\cdots+|r_{l}|+|s|\leq k
and
|r_{m}|\geq 1
for all
m
.

It is convenient to write
k_{m}:=|r_{m}|
and
k_{0}=|s|
.

First assume that
L+1-k_{m}<\frac{d-2}{2}
for all
m\geq 1
.
Then by the Sobolev embedding theorem and interpolation, for
m=1,\ldots,l
,

\|\partial^{r_{m}}q\|_{L^{p_{m}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\|\partial^{r_{m}}q\|_{H^{L+1-k_{m}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\|\nabla q\|_{H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\Theta\\
\mbox{ for all $p_{m}$ such that }\frac{1}{2}\geq p_{m}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{L+1-k_{m}}{d-2}.

(A.59)

Since the function
y_{a}\mapsto(1+|y_{a}|)^{-1}
belongs to
L^{p_{m}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
for
p_{m}>2
, it follows that

\|Q_{r_{m}}\|_{L^{p_{m}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\Theta\quad\mbox{ if }\ \ \frac{1}{2}>p_{m}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{L+1-k_{m}}{d-2}.

Similarly, if
k-k_{0}-1<\frac{d}{2}
, then

\|\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\qquad\mbox{ if }\ \ \frac{1}{2}\geq\frac{1}{p_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k-k_{0}-1}{d}.

We observe that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k-k_{0}-1}{d}\right)+\sum_{m=1}^{l}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{L+1-k_{m}}{d-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k-k_{0}-1}{d}\right)+\sum_{m=1}^{l}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{L+1-k_{m}}{d}\right)

\displaystyle=(l+1)\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{d}\right)+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{m=0}^{l}{k_{m}}+\frac{1}{d}(1-l(L+1-k)).

Since
\sum_{m=0}^{l}k_{m}\leq k
and
k>\frac{d}{2}
, and because
l\geq 1
and
k\leq L
, the right-hand side is less than
1/2
.
Thus we can choose
p_{0},\ldots,p_{l}
such that
\frac{1}{p_{0}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{p_{l}}=\frac{1}{2}
.
Then Hölder’s inequality implies that

\|\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\prod_{m=1}^{l}Q_{r_{m}}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\|\partial^{s}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\prod_{m=1}^{l}\left\|Q_{r_{m}}\right\|_{L^{p_{m}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\Theta^{l}\|\tilde{u}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.60)

If
L+1-k_{m}\geq(d-2)/2
for some
m\geq 1
or if
k-k_{0}-1\geq\frac{d}{2}
, then the corresponding exponents
p_{m}
may be chosen to be
+\infty
if strict inequality holds,
and arbitrarily close to
+\infty
otherwise, and one can again arrange that
\frac{1}{p_{0}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{p_{m}}=\frac{1}{2}
,
again leading to (
A.60
). We deduce (
A.56
) from (
A.58
) and (
A.60
).

To prove (
A.55
), we fix
|r|\leq k
and compute
\partial^{r}(f(q)\tilde{u})
. The terms that arise can all be estimated by (
A.56
), unless
|r|=k
, in which case we obtain a term
f(q)\partial^{r}\tilde{u}
that can be estimated easily using the fact that
f(q)\in L^{\infty}
.

The proof of (
A.57
) is very similar to that of (
A.56
) and is omitted.
∎

Lemma 25
.

Assume that
q:{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\times(0,T)\to M_{N}
satisfies (
1.9
) and (
1.10
),
with

\Theta:=\|\partial_{\bar{a}}q(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}+\|\partial_{0}q(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}<\infty.

Assume that
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times M_{N}\to{\mathbb{R}}
or
{\mathbb{C}}
is a function such that

|\partial^{r}f(x,q)|\leq Ce^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}\qquad\mbox{ whenever $q\in K$ and $|r|\leq L$},

(A.61)

for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
, with
C=C(r,K,\gamma)
, and define
f(q)
is in (
A.53
).

Then for any
\alpha,\beta\in 0,3,\ldots.d
, and for
j=\lfloor\frac{d}{2}\rfloor>\frac{d}{2}-1
,

\|\partial_{\alpha}q\,f(q)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W_{\gamma}^{L-j,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\ +\ \|\partial_{\alpha}q\,f(q)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C

(A.62)

and

\|\partial_{\alpha}q\,\partial_{\beta}q\,f(q)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{L-j,\infty}_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\ +\ \|\partial_{\alpha}q\,\partial_{\beta}q\,f(q)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C

(A.63)

for a constant depending on
C(r,K,\gamma)
above as well as
\Theta,L,j
.

Proof.

Since
j>\frac{d}{2}-1
, a Sobolev embedding implies that
\partial_{\alpha}q(\cdot,t)\in H^{L}\hookrightarrow W^{L-j,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})
for every
t\in(0,T)
, with the estimate

\|\partial_{\alpha}q\,(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}W^{L-j,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}\leq C\Theta
. This and the previous lemma easily imply the
W^{L-j,\infty}
estimates in (
A.62
), (
A.63
).

To prove the
H^{L}
estimates, we consider only (
A.63
), which is the slightly harder case. We fix
t\in(0,T)
and suppress the
t
variable. We next fix
|r|\leq L
and expand as in (
A.58
).
A typical term on the right-hand side has the form

C|\partial^{r_{1}}(\partial_{\alpha}q\,\partial_{\beta}q)|\prod_{m=2}^{l}|\partial^{r_{m}}q|(y_{\bar{a}})e^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}

(A.64)

where
|r_{1}|+\cdots+|r_{m}|\leq L
. We write
|r_{j}|=k_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,l
.
Since
L>\frac{d}{2}-1
, the space
H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})
is an algebra, and

\|\partial^{r_{1}}(\partial_{\alpha}q\,\partial_{\beta}q)\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\leq C\|\partial_{\alpha}q\,\partial_{\beta}q\|_{H^{L}}\leq C\|\partial_{\alpha}q\|_{H^{L}}\|\partial_{\beta}q\|_{H^{L}}\leq C\Theta^{2}

as long as

\frac{1}{2}\geq\frac{1}{p_{1}}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{L-k_{1}}{d-2}

and
L-k_{1}<\frac{d}{2}-1
, with straightforward adjustments if
L-k_{1}\geq\frac{d}{2}-1
. With this observation, the verification that (
A.64
) belongs to
L^{2}
is very similar to the proof of (
A.56
) and is omitted.
∎

Lemma 26
.

Assume that
L+1>\frac{d}{2}
and that
q:{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\to M_{N}
satisfies (
A.51
), with

\partial_{\bar{a}}q\in H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})
.

Assume that
F\in H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d};{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
for some
k\leq L
, and
for
\mu\in\{1,\ldots,2N\}

define
f_{\mu}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

f_{\mu}(y_{\bar{a}})=(F,n_{\mu}(q))_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{y_{\bar{a}}})}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}F(y_{a},y_{\bar{a}})\cdot n_{\mu}(y_{a};q(y_{\bar{a}}))\,dy_{a}.

Then
f_{\mu}\in H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})
, and

\|f_{\mu}\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2)}}\leq C\|F\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(A.65)

for
C
depending on
\|\partial_{\bar{a}}q\|_{H^{L}}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
F
is smooth, since the general case then follows by an approximation argument. Let
r
be a multiindex on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}
, so that
\partial^{r}
involves only derivatives with respect to the
y_{\bar{a}}
directions. Assume that
|r|\leq k
. Since
F
is smooth, it is straightforward to justify the identity

\partial^{r}f(y_{\bar{a}})=\sum_{r_{0}+s_{0}=r}\frac{r!}{r_{0}!s_{0}!}(\partial^{r_{0}}F,\partial^{s_{0}}n_{\mu})_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{y_{a}})}.

In view of Lemma
4
and the chain rule, there exists
\gamma>0
such that

|\partial^{r_{0}}F\cdot\partial^{s_{0}}n_{\mu}|(y)\leq C|\partial^{r_{0}}F(y)|\,\left(\sum_{r_{1}+\cdots+r_{l}=s_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{l}|\partial^{r_{j}}q(y_{\bar{a}})|\right)e^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}.

Writing

G(y_{\bar{a}}):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{y_{\bar{a}}}}|\partial^{r_{0}}F(y)|\,e^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}dy_{a},

we deduce that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}|(\partial^{r_{0}}F,\partial^{s_{0}}n_{\mu})_{L^{2}_{y_{a}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}|^{2}dy_{\bar{a}}\leq C\sum_{r_{1}+\cdots+r_{l}=s_{0}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}G(y_{\bar{a}})^{2}\prod_{j=1}^{l}|\partial^{r_{j}}q(y_{\bar{a}})|^{2}\ dy_{\bar{a}}.

Arguing exactly as in the proof of Lemma
24
, and using the assumptions that
L+1>\frac{d}{2}
and
k\leq L
, we can choose

p_{j}\in[1,\infty]
for
j=0,\ldots,l
such that

\|\partial^{r_{0}}F\|_{L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|F\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\qquad\|\partial^{r_{j}}q\|_{L^{p_{j}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-1})}\leq C\|\partial_{\bar{a}}q\|_{H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})},

and

\frac{2}{p_{0}}+\cdots+\frac{2}{p_{l}}=1.

We use Hölder’s inequality with exponents
\frac{p_{j}}{2}
for
j=0,\ldots,l
to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}|(\partial^{r_{0}}F,\partial^{s_{0}}n_{\mu})_{L^{2}_{y_{a}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}|^{2}dy_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq C\sum_{r_{1}+\cdots+r_{l}=s_{0}}\|G\|_{L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}^{2}\prod_{j=1^{l}}\|\partial^{r_{j}}q\|_{L^{p_{j}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}^{2}.

Note, we define
q_{0}
by requiring that
\frac{1}{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=1
, and we again use Hölder’s inequality to deduce that

\displaystyle\|G\|_{L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}|\partial^{r_{0}}F(y)|\ e^{-\gamma|y_{a}|}dy_{a}\right)^{p_{0}}dy_{\bar{a}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-2}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}|\partial^{r_{0}}F(y)|^{p_{0}}dy_{a}\right)\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\gamma q_{0}|y_{a}|}dy_{a}\right)^{p_{0}/q_{0}}dy_{\bar{a}}\right]^{1/p_{0}}

\displaystyle=C\|\partial^{r_{0}}F\|_{L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

Putting these together, we conclude that

\|\partial^{r}f\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})}^{2}\leq C(\|\partial_{\bar{a}}q\|_{H^{L}({\mathbb{R}}^{d-2})})\|F\|_{H^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}.

Since this holds for every
r
such that
|r|\leq k
, the conclusion follows.

∎
